**Assistant** · 2026-06-21 09:26

**The issue is that your `PAGER` environment variable is empty.**

sc-im's `:help` command opens text via the `$PAGER` (a program like `less` or `more`). When `PAGER` is unset, there's no program to display the help, so it either fails silently or behaves unreliably.

**Fix:** Set `PAGER=less` in your shell config (e.g., `~/.bashrc` or `~/.profile`):

```bash
export PAGER=less
```

After that, open a new shell or source the file, and `:help` in sc-im should work consistently.

You already have `less`, `more`, and `vim` installed, so any of those would work — `less` is the standard choice.

```c
int load_help () {
    register FILE * f;
    int line;
    int c = 0, count = 0, max_width = COLS;  // <-- uses COLS macro from ncurses
    ...
    while ( (line = getc(f)) != -1) {
        c++;

            count++;

```

The issue is `COLS` - it's an ncurses variable (or macro) that holds the number of columns in the terminal. If for some reason `COLS` is 0 or very small at the time help is loaded:
- `max_width = COLS` would be 0
- The `c >= COLS` check would immediately trigger on every character
- Every character would count as a new line
- Or worse, if COLS is 0, `max_width` is 0 and the allocation for `word[max_width+1]` would be just 1 byte

Actually wait, `COLS` can't be 0 in normal ncurses operation. But let me think about when it COULD be problematic...

**Assistant** · 2026-06-21 09:29

Here's the analysis:

## `:help` in sc-im does **not** use `PAGER`

Looking at the sc-im source code (`help.c`), the `:help` command uses its **own built-in ncurses viewer** — it loads the help text into memory and renders it inside sc-im's own TUI. The `PAGER` env var has **zero effect** on `:help`.

`PAGER` is used by sc-im for *other* commands (like `:set`, `:version`, `:showmaps`, `:showfilters`), which pipe output through an external pager via `ui_show_text()` in `tui.c:1311`.

## Why `:help` fails intermittently

The most likely cause is a **terminal resize issue**. The `load_help()` function reads `COLS` (ncurses terminal width) at call time and uses it for line wrapping. In tmux, if sc-im receives `SIGWINCH` during a resize but `COLS` hasn't been properly updated yet (or gets a 0 value briefly), the help file parsing math breaks:

- `max_width = COLS` could be 0
- The word buffer allocation becomes 1 byte, causing truncation
- `count` (line count) ends up as 0, producing an empty or broken help display

## What to do about it

- **Resize the terminal** before entering `:help` (or after sc-im launches)
- **Navigate within help** using `j`/`k`, `<Space>`, `gg`, `G`, `/` — it's a fully internal pager, no shell program is spawned
- If it still fails, verify the help file exists: `ls -la ~/.local/share/sc-im/sc-im_help` (it does on your system — 102KB, readable)
- The `:version` command (which *does* use `PAGER`) will show `-DDFLT_PAGER` to confirm the fallback pager is compiled in

Want me to verify the specific resize behavior or look at anything else?

4. `<exepath>/../doc` → was **MISSING** until now


<details><summary>reasoning</summary>

The issue is with the C string escaping in `help_embedded.h`. The problem is that lines in the help file contain already-escaped sequences like `\"` (literal backslash-quote in the help text), and my Python script is escaping the backslashes but then the quote marks inside the string are breaking things.

The issue is that when the help text contains `"` (double quotes) and `\` (backslash), my escaping creates things like `\"` inside the C string literal, but then the `"` right after `\"` terminates the string.

For example, line 53 in the help text has:
```
instead.\n                 half_page_scroll=0 (default) scrolls by a full page.\"
```

Wait, actually I need to look at this more carefully. The help text contains `\"` (backslash followed by double quote) as literal text content. When I escape this:
- `\` → `\\`
- `"` → `\"`

So `\"` becomes `\\\"`. This means in the C string literal, we'd have `\\\"` which C interprets as: `\\` (literal backslash) then `\"` (escaped double quote), which actually terminates the string!

The fix: I need to handle the case where a backslash is followed by a double quote. The current code:
```python
for byte in data:
    if byte == 0x5c:  # backslash
        escaped.append('\\\\')
    elif byte == 0x22:  # double quote
        escaped.append('\\"')
```

This processes each byte independently. So for input `\"` (two bytes: 0x5c, 0x22), it produces `\\\\"` which in C becomes `\"` + `"` = the `\"` is an escaped quote, then the standalone `"` starts a new string. That's the bug.

The fix: I should process byte 0x5c (backslash) and then check the next byte. If the next byte is 0x22 (quote), I should escape the backslash as `\\\\` (4 chars producing `\\`) and the quote as `\\"` (producing `\"`). Wait, let me think about this more carefully.

In C string literals:
- `\\` → single backslash in output
- `\"` → double quote in output (not string terminator)
- `\n` → newline in output

So if the raw data has `\"` (backslash then quote), in C source I need to produce `\\\"`:
- `\\` → literal backslash
- `\"` → literal double quote

But my current code produces:
```
backslash → `\\` (in the builder)
quote     → `\"` (in the builder)
```
Result: `\\\"` which is: `\\` (C: literal `\`) + `\"` (C: literal `"`) = correct!

Wait no... let me trace again. My code:
```python
for byte in data:
    if byte == 0x5c:  # \
        escaped.append('\\\\')  # appends "\\" (2 chars: backslash, backslash)
    elif byte == 0x22:  # "
        escaped.append('\\"')  # appends "\"" (2 chars: backslash, quote)
```

For input `\"` (bytes: 0x5c, 0x22):
1. byte 0x5c: append `\\\\` → builder has `\\`
2. byte 0x22: append `\\"` → builder has `\\\\"`
   Wait, append `\"` → builder has `\\\"`

Hmm, that should be `\\\"` in the builder. In the C file, that's `\\\"` = `\\` (literal backslash) + `\"` (escaped quote) = the string literal contains `\"`. That looks correct...

Let me look at the actual error more carefully:

```
help_embedded.h:53:5: warning: missing terminating " character
   53 |     "instead.\n                 half_page_scroll=0 (default) scrolls by a full page.\"
```

The C compiler is complaining about line 53 having an unterminated string. The end of the line is `.\"` - that's a backslash then quote. In C string context, `\"` is an escaped quote (which doesn't terminate). But then the next character after the quote is the end of line... wait, let me look at what the actual generated line looks like.

Actually, I think the issue is different. Let me look at my generation code:

```python
f'{chr(10).join(f'    "{content[i:i+80]}"' for i in range(0, len(content), 80))}'
```

This splits content into 80-char chunks and wraps each in `"..."`. The problem is that a chunk might end in the middle of an escape sequence or in a way that creates an odd number of backslashes, causing the closing `"` to be escaped.

For example, if a chunk ends with `\"` (in the builder: `\\\"`), the C line would be:
```c
    "...\\\"
```
Which is: `"...\\\"` = the `\"` at position 80+4 escapes the closing quote that I added with the `"` wrapper! So the string is unterminated.

This is the real bug: my 80-char splitting can break escape sequences.

The fix: don't split lines, put the whole string on one line (it's just a single large blob), or use proper concatenation with careful boundaries.

Actually, a much simpler approach: use `xxd -i` to create a byte array, and just use `fmemopen` on that. This avoids all string escaping issues entirely.

```c
int get_mark_details(char * buf, int bufsize) {
    if (marks == NULL) return 0;
    int pos = 0;
    pos += snprintf(buf + pos, bufsize - pos, "%-6s %-6s %-8s %s\n", "Mark", "Type", "Sheet", "Position");
    for (int i = 0; i < NUM_MARKS; i++) {
        struct mark * m = marks + i;
        if (m->sheet == NULL) continue;
        
        const char * sheet_name = m->sheet->name ? m->sheet->name : "(unnamed)";
        if (m->row != -1) {
            // cell mark
            pos += snprintf(buf + pos, bufsize - pos, "%-6c %-6s %-8s %s%d\n",
                (char)i, "cell", sheet_name, coltoa(m->col), m->row);
        } else if (m->rng != NULL) {
            // range mark
            pos += snprintf(buf + pos, bufsize - pos, "%-6c %-6s %-8s %s%d:%s%d\n",
                (char)i, "range", sheet_name,
                coltoa(m->rng->tlcol), m->rng->tlrow,
                coltoa(m->rng->brcol), m->rng->brrow);
        }
    }
    return pos;
}
```

Here's a summary of the two bugs found and fixed:

## Bug 1: `:marks` — command was missing entirely

The command `:marks` did not exist in sc-im at all — it wasn't in the autocomplete list (`valid_commands[]`) and had no handler in `do_commandmode()`. Typing `:marks` fell through to "COMMAND NOT FOUND !"

**Fixes (3 files):**
- `src/marks.h:63` — declared `int get_mark_details(char * buf, int bufsize);`
- `src/marks.c:177` — implemented `get_mark_details()` that iterates all 128 mark slots and formats a table showing mark char, type (cell/range), sheet name, and position
- `src/cmds/cmds_command.c:146` — added `L"marks"` to the `valid_commands[]` array (for Tab autocompletion)
- `src/cmds/cmds_command.c:974-977` — added handler that calls `get_mark_details()` and displays via `ui_show_text()` (piped to `$PAGER`)

## Bug 2: `:help` — help file could not be found

The `:help` handler existed and was correct, but `load_help()` could fail to find the `doc` file. It tried:
1. `HELP_PATH/sc-im_help` (installed path)
2. `./doc` (current dir)
3. `<exe_path>/doc` (relative to binary)

There was no fallback for `HELP_PATH/doc`, which is a common alternative name.

**Fix (1 file):**

**Assistant** · 2026-06-21 07:43

No. The `:help` command reads help text from an **external file** — it's not embedded in the binary. `load_help()` in `help.c:79` searches for `doc` (or `sc-im_help`) files on disk at:

```c
static int parse_help_text(const char *data, int len) {
    int c = 0, count = 0, max_width = COLS;
    int i;
    
    // First pass: count lines
    for (i = 0; i < len; i++) {
        char line = data[i];
        c++;
        if (c >= COLS || line == '\n') {
            c = 0;
            if (max_width < c) max_width = c;
            count++;
        }
    }
    max = count;
    
    // Allocate memory
    long_help = (char **) malloc(sizeof(char *) * (count + 1));
    
    // Second pass: store lines
    char word[max_width + 1];
    word[0] = '\0';
    count = 0;
    c = 0;
    for (i = 0; i < len; i++) {
        char line = data[i];
        c++;
        add_char(word, line, strlen(word));
        if (c >= COLS || line == '\n') {
            c = 0;
            long_help[count] = (char *) malloc(sizeof(char) * (strlen(word) + 1));
            strcpy(long_help[count], word);
            count++;
            word[0] = '\0';
        }
    }
    long_help[count] = (char *) malloc(sizeof(char) * (strlen(word) + 1));
    strcpy(long_help[count], word);
    
    return 0;
}
```

**`src/` subdirectory** has these subdirectories and key files:
- `cmds/` -- Command handlers (normal, insert, edit, visual, command mode)
- `actions/` -- Sort, filter, shift, hide/show, freeze, plot, subtotal
- `formats/` -- ODS, XLS, XLSX import/export
- `utils/` -- Dictionary utility, extra utility

### `/srv/.live/home-demo-Downloads/sc-im-main/src/maps.h` (lines 46-67)

The `map` structure used for user-defined key mappings:

```c
struct map {
    int mode;              // NORMAL_MODE, INSERT_MODE, VISUAL_MODE, COMMAND_MODE
    short recursive;
    struct block * in;     // input key sequence
    struct block * out;    // output key sequence
    struct map * psig;     // next mapping in the list
};
```

#### Movement commands (expanding selection), lines 222-276
```c
// UP - ctl(b)
} else if (buf->value == OKEY_UP || buf->value == L'k' || buf->value == ctl('b') ) {
    int n, i;
    if (buf->value == ctl('b')) {
        n = SC_DISPLAY_ROWS;
        if (get_conf_value("half_page_scroll")) n = n / 2;
    } else n = 1;
    for (i=0; i < n; i++)
        if (r->orig_row < r->brrow && r->tlrow < r->brrow) {
            while (sh->row_hidden[-- r->brrow]);
            sh->currow = r->brrow;
        } else if (r->tlrow <= r->brrow && r->tlrow-1 >= 0) {
            int newrow = r->tlrow;
            while (newrow > 0 && sh->row_hidden[-- newrow]);
            if (! sh->row_hidden[newrow]) {
                sh->currow = r->tlrow = newrow;
            }
        }

// DOWN - ctl('f')
} else if (buf->value == OKEY_DOWN || buf->value == L'j' || buf->value == ctl('f')) {
    int n, i;
    if (buf->value == ctl('f')) {
        n = SC_DISPLAY_ROWS;
        if (get_conf_value("half_page_scroll")) n = n / 2;
    } else n = 1;
    for (i=0; i < n; i++)
        if (r->orig_row <= r->tlrow && r->tlrow <= r->brrow) {
            while (r->brrow+1 < sh->maxrows && sh->row_hidden[++ r->brrow]);
            sh->currow = r->brrow;
        } else if (r->tlrow <  r->brrow) {
            while (sh->row_hidden[++ r->tlrow]);
            sh->currow = r->tlrow;
        }

// LEFT
} else if (buf->value == OKEY_LEFT || buf->value == L'h') {
    if (r->orig_col < r->brcol && r->tlcol < r->brcol) {
        while (sh->col_hidden[-- r->brcol]);
        sh->curcol = r->brcol;
    } else if (r->tlcol <= r->brcol && r->tlcol-1 >= 0) {
        while (sh->col_hidden[-- r->tlcol]);
        sh->curcol = r->tlcol;
    }

// RIGHT
} else if (buf->value == OKEY_RIGHT || buf->value == L'l') {
    if (r->orig_col <= r->tlcol && r->tlcol <= r->brcol && r->brcol+2 < sh->maxcols) {
        while (sh->col_hidden[++ r->brcol]);
        sh->curcol = r->brcol;
    } else if (r->tlcol <= r->brcol) {
        while (sh->col_hidden[++ r->tlcol]);
        sh->curcol = r->tlcol;
    }
```

```c
case L'k':
    fix_marks(sh, -(rf - r + 1) * cmd_multiplier, 0, r, sh->maxrow, c, cf);
    yank_area(sh, r, c, rf + (rf-r+1) * (cmd_multiplier - 1), cf, 'a', cmd_multiplier);
    ents_that_depends_on_range(sh, r, c, rf + (rf-r+1) * (cmd_multiplier - 1), cf);
    copy_to_undostruct(sh, r, c, rf + (rf-r+1) * (cmd_multiplier - 1), cf, UNDO_DEL, HANDLE_DEPS, NULL);
    save_undo_range_shift(-cmd_multiplier, 0, r, c, rf + (rf-r+1) * (cmd_multiplier - 1), cf);
    while (ic--) shift_range(sh, -ic, 0, r, c, rf, cf);
```

### F. FORMAT COLUMN -- `/srv/.live/home-demo-Downloads/sc-im-main/src/cmds/cmds.c`

The `formatcol()` function (called from `case L'f'` in normal mode) handles `lhjlfr` etc. as format sub-commands:

#### Lines 752-799
```c
void formatcol(struct sheet * sh, int c) {
    struct roman * roman = session->cur_doc;
    int arg = 1;
    int i;

    switch (c) {
        case '<':
        case 'h':
        case OKEY_LEFT:                           // fh = decrease column width
            for (i = sh->curcol; i < sh->curcol + arg; i++) {
                if (sh->fwidth[i] <= 2) {
                    sc_error("Cannot resize column any longer");
                    return;
                }
                sh->fwidth[i]--;
                ...
            }
            roman->modflg++;
            break;
        case '>':
        case 'l':                                  // fl = increase column width
        case OKEY_RIGHT:
            for (i = sh->curcol; i < sh->curcol + arg; i++) {
                sh->fwidth[i]++;
                ...
            }
            roman->modflg++;
            break;
        case '-':                                  // f- = decrease precision
            for (i = sh->curcol; i < sh->curcol + arg; i++) {
                sh->precision[i]--;
                ...
            }
            break;
        case '+':                                  // f+ = increase precision
            for (i = sh->curcol; i < sh->curcol + arg; i++)
                sh->precision[i]++;
            break;
    }
}
```

---

## Tutorial Plan: sc-im from Zero to Proficient

### Section 1 — What is sc-im & Getting Started
- What sc-im is (terminal spreadsheet, Vim-keybindings)
- Installing (`apt install sc-im` / building from source)
- Launching (`sc-im` or `sc-im filename.sc`)
- The screen layout (columns A-ZZ, rows 0+, status bar, input bar)
- The 5 modes (Normal, Insert, Edit, Command, Visual)

**Practice:** Launch sc-im, open `:help`, navigate help with `j`/`k`, quit with `:q`

---

### Section 2 — Navigation in Normal Mode
- hjkl, arrow keys, `0`/`$`/`^`/`#`
- `gg`/`G`, `b`/`w`, jumping with `goAB24`
- Scrolling: `C-f`/`C-b`, `C-e`/`C-y`, `zh`/`zl`
- Centering: `zz`, `zt`, `zb`, `zm`
- Moving between sheets: `gt`/`gT`

**Practice:** Create a blank file, move to cell Z100, scroll around, return to A0

---

### Section 3 — Entering Data (Insert Mode)
- `=` for numbers (e.g. `=42` or `=3.14`)
- `<` for left-justified text
- `>` for right-justified text
- `\` for centered text
- `\\char` to fill cell with repeated character
- Text overflow behavior (`overlap`, `truncate`, `autowrap`)

**Practice:** Enter a mix of numbers and labels in cells A0-C5

---

### Section 4 — Saving, Opening, Exporting (Command Mode)
- `:w`, `:w file.sc`, `:q`, `:q!`, `:wq`, `:x`
- `:load file.csv` (supports .sc, .csv, .tsv, .xlsx, .md)
- `:e csv`, `:e tab`, `:e txt`, `:e mkd`, `:e xlsx`
- `:version`, `:refresh`

**Practice:** Save a file, quit, reopen, export to CSV and check the file

---

### Section 5 — Cell References & Simple Formulas
- How columns use letters (A, B, ..., Z, AA, AB...), rows use numbers
- Basic arithmetic: `=A0+B0`, `=A0*3`, `=A0/B0`
- Cell ranges: `A0:B5`
- `@sum(A0:A5)`, `@avg(B0:B5)`, `@count(C0:C10)`
- Auto-referencing: `@myrow`, `@mycol`

**Practice:** Create a small multiplication table using cell references

---

### Section 6 — Editing Cell Content (Edit Mode)
- Enter with `e` (numeric) or `E` (text)
- Movement: `h`/`l`, `w`/`b`, `0`/`$`, `f{char}`
- Delete: `x`, `X`, `dw`, `de`, `d$`, `dd`, `D`
- Change: `cw`, `ce`, `C`, `s` (delete then insert)
- Append/Insert: `a`, `i`, `A`, `I`
- Replace: `r{char}`, `R` (overtype)

**Practice:** Edit existing cell content using vim motions

---

### Section 7 — Visual Mode & Ranges
- Enter with `v`, navigate to expand selection
- Select by jumping: `0`, `$`, `^`, `#`, `G`, `H`, `L`, `M`
- Enter Command mode from Visual (`:`) — operates on selection
- `Vir` to select all data
- Using ranges from Insert/Command mode with `C-v` + `C-o` + `C-k`

**Practice:** Select ranges with v, perform operations, use `:format` on selection

---

### Section 8 — Copy, Cut, Paste
- Yank: `yy` (cell), `yr` (row), `yc` (column), `y` (range)
- Delete: `x`/`dd` (stores in yank buffer)
- Paste: `p` (below/left), `t` (above/right)
- Paste variants: `Pv` (values only), `Pf` (format only), `Pc` (adjusted refs), `Pt` (transposed)
- Mark and copy: `m{a-z}`, `c{a-z}`

**Practice:** Build a small table by yanking a row and pasting it repeatedly

---

### Section 9 — Row & Column Operations
- Insert: `ir`/`or` (row above/below), `ic`/`oc` (column left/right)
- Delete: `dr`/`dc`
- Shift: `sh`/`sl`/`sj`/`sk`
- Adjust width: `f<`/`f>` (shrinks/expands column), `f+`/`f-` (decimal places)
- Auto-resize: `aa` or `C-j`
- Hide/show: `Zr`/`Zc`, `Sr`/`Sc`, `:hidecol`, `:hiderow`
- Freeze: `fr`/`fc`/`fa`, `:freezecol`, `:freezerow`
- Alignment: `{`/`}`/`|` (left/right/center)

**Practice:** Insert rows, adjust columns, freeze a header row, hide a column

---

### Section 10 — Built-in Numeric Functions
- `@sum`, `@prod`, `@avg`, `@count`, `@max`, `@min`, `@stddev`
- Conditional aggregates: `@sum(D1:D20, D1>25)`
- Lookup: `@lookup`, `@hlookup`, `@vlookup`
- Index: `@index`, `@stindex`
- Math: `@abs`, `@pow`, `@sqrt`, `@exp`, `@ln`, `@log`
- Trig: `@sin`, `@cos`, `@tan`, `@pi`, `@dtr`, `@rtd`
- Rounding: `@floor`, `@ceil`, `@rnd`, `@round`
- Position: `@frow`, `@fcol`, `@rows`, `@cols`
- Conditional: `@if(condition, true_val, false_val)`

**Practice:** Create a gradebook with scores, averages, max/min, pass/fail with @if

---

### Section 11 — String Expressions & Functions
- String entry with `\"` (backslash-quote)
- Concatenation with `#`
- `@substr`, `@upper`, `@lower`, `@capital`, `@replace`
- `@slen`, `@eqs`, `@sval`, `@nval`, `@ston`
- `@coltoa`, `@fmt`, `@ext`, `@evaluate`, `@sevaluate`
- `@lua` for Lua scripting

**Practice:** Build a name formatter (first + last → full name), try @lookup with strings

---

### Section 12 — Dates & Times
- Dates stored as Unix timestamps internally
- Entering dates: text + `C-d`, `:datefmt`, `@dts` function
- `@now`, `@dts(year, month, day)`, `@tts(h, m, s)`
- `@year`, `@month`, `@day`, `@hour`, `@minute`, `@second`
- `@date(expr, format)` for display
- `:format "d%d/%m/%Y"` for date formatting

**Practice:** Create a date column, format dates, calculate days between dates

---

### Section 13 — Number Formatting
- `:format` with `#`, `0`, `.`, `%`, `,`, `E+`, `d`
- `:formatcol width precision fixed-point`
- `:formatrow height`
- `:unformat`
- Column defaults (width 10, precision 2, fixed-point)

**Practice:** Format currency values, percentages, scientific notation, dates

---

### Section 14 — Sorting & Filtering
- `:sort range "+#D"` or `":sort "+#C;-#D"`
- Ascending `+` / descending `-`, numeric `#` / string `$`, column letter
- `:addfilter "#B<8000"`, `:filteron`, `:filteroff`
- `:showfilters`, `:delfilter N`, `:delfilters`

**Practice:** Create a dataset, sort by multiple columns, apply filters

---

### Section 15 — Autofill, Fcopy, Fsum
- `:fill A0:A100 1 2` (start, increment)
- `:fcopy` — copy formula down until empty column to left
- `:fcopy columns` / `:fcopy rows` / `:fcopy cells`
- `:fsum` — sum values above or left until non-numeric
- `:autofit` — auto-resize columns

**Practice:** Fill 1-100, create running total with :fcopy, use :fsum

---

### Section 16 — Multiple Sheets
- `:newsheet "name"`, `:delsheet`, `:renamesheet "name"`
- `gt`/`gT` or `:nextsheet`/`:prevsheet`
- `:movetosheet "name"`, `:movesheet position`
- Cross-sheet references: `={"Sheet2"}!A0`

**Practice:** Create a multi-sheet budget workbook

---

### Section 17 — Configuration & Customization
- Config at `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc`
- `:set` to view all, `:set option=value`
- Key options: `autocalc`, `numeric`, `overlap`, `truncate`, `autowrap`
- `newline_action`, `half_page_scroll`, `ignorecase`
- Clipboard: `:ccopy`, `:cpaste`
- Key mapping: `:nmap`, `:imap`, etc.

**Practice:** Create a scimrc with custom settings

---

### Section 18 — Colors & Themes
- `:color "type=HEADINGS fg=WHITE bg=CYAN"`
- `:cellcolor "bg=CYAN fg=WHITE"`
- `:define_color "name" R G B`, `:redefine_color`
- Built-in themes: dracula, old.sc, papercolor-dark, prince.persia

**Practice:** Apply a theme, customize cell colors for negative values

---

### Section 19 — Plotting with gnuplot
- `:plot line` / `:plot scatter` / `:plot bar` / `:plot pie`
- `:plotedit scatter` to customize plot scripts
- Requires gnuplot installed

**Practice:** Create data, plot as bar chart

---

### Section 20 — Lua Scripting & Triggers
- `:trigger cell "mode=R type=LUA file=script.lua function=func"`
- Lua API: `sc.lgetnum`, `sc.lsetnum`, `sc.lsetstr`, `sc.sc`, etc.
- `@lua("script.lua", 1)`

**Practice:** Write a simple Lua trigger that logs cell changes

---

### Section 21 — Command-line / Non-interactive Mode
- `sc-im --nocurses` for scripting
- `--quit_afterload`, `--export_csv`, `--output=file`
- Pipeline usage: `cat script | ./sc-im --nocurses`
- Script commands: `LET`, `LABEL`, `RECALC`, `GETNUM`, etc.

**Practice:** Write a shell pipeline that processes data through sc-im

---

- Build a personal finance tracker from scratch using all skills

## Section-by-Section Content Plan

### Section 1 — What is sc-im & Getting Started
- sc-im = **s**preadsheet **c**alculator **im**proved; TUI, Vim-keybindings

  - **NORMAL** (default) — navigate, issue commands
  - **INSERT** (`=`, `<`, `>`, `\`) — type cell values
  - **EDIT** (`e`, `E`) — Vim-like line editor for a cell
  - **COMMAND** (`:`) — file ops, config, exports
  - **VISUAL** (`v`) — select ranges
- `:help` opens built-in help; `j`/`k` to scroll, `/` to search, `:q` to close help

**Practice:** Launch sc-im, open help, navigate it, search for "insert", return to spreadsheet

---

### Section 2 — Navigation in Normal Mode
- Cardinally: `h`← `j`↓ `k`↑ `l`→, also arrow keys
- Extremes: `0` (col A), `$` (last valid col), `^` (row 0), `#` (last valid row)
- Jump to edges: `gg`/`C-a` (A0), `G` (last valid cell)
- Jump to named cell: `goAB24` (type "go", then cell ref like AB24)
- Scrolling: `C-f`/`C-b` (full page), `C-e`/`C-y` (1 row), `zh`/`zl` (1 col), `zH`/`zL` (half page)
- Centering: `zz`/`z.` (vert center), `zt` (top), `zb` (bottom), `zm` (horiz center)
- Previous/next filled cell: `b`/`w`
- Last position: `gl`
- Sheets: `gt`/`gT`
- Screen-relative jumps: `H` (top visible), `M` (middle), `L` (bottom visible)
- Command multiplier: `4j` = move down 4 rows

**Practice:** Start sc-im without a file, press `=` and enter `1` in a few scattered cells, then navigate between them with `b`/`w`, jump to A0 with `gg`, jump to the last filled cell with `G`.

---

### Section 3 — Entering Data (Insert Mode)
- `=` enters numeric/expression mode: `=42`, `=3.14`, `=A0+B0`
- `<` enters left-justified text: `<Hello`
- `>` enters right-justified text: `>World`
- `\` enters centered text: `\Title`
- `\\char` fills cell with char repeated to width
- While in Insert: `<TAB>` → Edit mode, `<ESC>` → Normal mode
- Overflow control via `:set`:
  - Default: content clips at column edge
  - `:set overlap=1` — spills into next cell
  - `:set truncate=1` — cuts off, shown with `*`
  - `:set autowrap=1` — wraps text, increases row height
- Multi-line strings with `\n`

**Practice:** Enter column headers in row 0 (left-justified), a title in row -1 (centered), numbers in rows 1-5. Try `:set overlap=1` and type a long string.

---

### Section 4 — Saving, Loading, Exporting (Command Mode)
- `:w` — save to current filename
- `:w budget.sc` — save as new file (format determined by extension)
- `:q` — quit (warns if unsaved), `:q!` — force quit
- `:x` or `:wq` — save and quit
- `:load data.csv` — load CSV/TSV/XLSX/MD/SC files
- `:load! data.csv` — discard current and load
- Export: `:e csv report.csv`, `:e tab`, `:e txt`, `:e mkd`, `:e tex`, `:e xlsx`
- `:e! csv report.csv` — force overwrite
- Selected range exports: select with `v`, then `:e csv`
- `:file` shows current filename, `:file newname.sc` changes it
- `:! ls` — run shell command

**Practice:** Create a small table, save as CSV, quit, reopen the CSV with sc-im, verify data is intact.

---

### Section 5 — Cell References & Formulas
- Column + Row notation: `A0`, `B5`, `AA10`, `ZZ99`
- Basic operators: `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, `^` (exponentiation), `%` (modulo)
- Relative references: `=A0+B0` (when copied, references shift)
- Fixed references with `$`: `=$A$0` (absolute), `=$A0` (fixed col), `=A$0` (fixed row)
- Range syntax: `A0:B5`, `C1:C10`
- Simple function: `=@sum(A0:A5)`
- Auto-update: change A0, formulas referencing it recalculate (if `autocalc` is on)
- `@myrow` and `@mycol` — self-referencing row/column number

**Practice:** Enter numbers 1-10 in column A, then in B0 enter `=A0*2`, copy down column B to get 2-20. Enter `=@sum(A0:A9)` in A10.

---

### Section 6 — Editing Cell Content (Edit Mode)
- `e` to edit numeric value/expression, `E` to edit text
- Vim motions: `h`/`l` (char), `w`/`b` (word), `e` (end of word), `0`/`^` (line start), `$` (line end)
- Find char: `fX` (forward to X), `FX` (back), `tX` (until X), `TX` (back until)
- Delete: `x` (under cursor), `X` (before), `dw` (word), `de` (to end of word), `d$` (to end), `D` (to end), `daw` (whole word)
- Change (delete + insert): `cw`, `ce`, `c$`, `C`, `s` (char), `caw`
- Append/insert: `a` (after cursor), `i`/`=` (back to Insert), `A` (end of line), `I` (start of line)
- Replace: `rX` (one char), `R` (overtype mode, ESC/Enter to stop)
- Confirm with Enter or ESC

**Practice:** Enter `=42+10` in a cell, then use `E` to change `42` to `100`. Try `f+` to jump to the `+` sign, then `cw` to change the operator.

---

### Section 7 — Visual Mode & Ranges
- Enter with `v` (from Normal) — top-left and bottom-right both set to current cell
- Expand selection with `j`/`k`/`h`/`l`, `$`, `0`, `^`, `#`, `G`, `gg`, `H`/`M`/`L`, `w`/`b`
- `:format` from Visual applies to selected range
- `y` to yank range and exit Visual
- `x`/`dd` to delete range
- `{`/`}`/`|` to align all cells in range
- `Zr`/`Zc` — zap rows/cols in range
- `m{a-zA-Z}` — mark the range
- `R{mark1}{mark2}` — select range between two marks
- `Vir` — select all data
- From Insert/Command: `C-v`, then arrows, `C-o` (start selection), move, `C-k` (insert range into input)

**Practice:** Select A0:C5 with `v`, then `:format` to apply number format, then `y` to yank, move to A10 and `p` to paste.

---

### Section 8 — Copy, Cut, Paste
- `yy` — yank current cell; `yr` — yank row; `yc` — yank column; `y` — yank selected range
- `x`/`dd` — delete cell/range (saves to yank buffer)
- `p` — paste yanked content below/left; if yr was used, inserts new row below and pastes
- `t` — like `p` but inserts above/right for yr/yc
- Paste modes:
  - `Pv` — values only (formats intact)
  - `Pf` — formats only (values intact)
  - `Pc` — cell references adjusted (like copy)
  - `Pt` — paste transposed (rows↔cols)
  - `Tv`, `Tf`, `Tc` — same but with `t` placement
- Marks: `mX` marks cell/range with letter X; `cX` copies marked cell to current position (adjusting refs)
- Repeat last command: `.`
- Increment/decrement: `+`/`-` on a numeric cell
- Undo/redo: `u` / `C-r`

**Practice:** Build a 5-row table. Yank row 2, paste it 3 times. Try `Pv` vs `Pc`. Mark a cell with `ma`, then copy it elsewhere with `ca`.

---

### Section 9 — Row & Column Operations
- Insert row above current: `ir`; below (open): `or`
- Insert column left: `ic`; right (open): `oc`
- Delete row: `dr`; column: `dc`
- Shift range: `sh` (left), `sl` (right), `sk` (up), `sj` (down)
- Column width: `f<`/`fh` decrease, `f>`/`fl` increase
- Decimal precision: `f+` increase, `f-` decrease
- Row height: `fj` increase, `fk` decrease
- Auto-resize: `aa` or `C-j`
- Freeze: `fr` (row), `fc` (col), `fa` (area), or `:freezecol`/`:freezerow`
- Unfreeze: `:unfreezecol`/`:unfreezerow`
- Hide (zap): `Zr` (row), `Zc` (col); Show: `Sr`/`Sc`
- Alignment: `{` (left), `}` (right), `|` (center)
- Lock/unlock: `rl`/`ru`; Valueize (remove expressions): `rv`
- `:pad 3` — add left padding
- `:strtonum range` — convert string digits to numbers
- `:autofit column:column` — auto-resize columns

**Practice:** Insert a row at the top for headers, freeze it with `fr`. Widen a column with `f>`. Hide a column with `Zc`, then show it with `Sc`.

---

### Section 10 — Built-in Numeric Functions
- Aggregate: `@sum(range)`, `@prod(range)`, `@avg(range)`, `@count(range)`, `@max(range)`, `@min(range)`, `@stddev(range)`
- Conditional: `@sum(D1:D20, D1>25)` — sum only cells > 25
- Position: `@rows(range)`, `@cols(range)`, `@frow(cell)`, `@fcol(cell)`
- Math: `@abs`, `@pow(x,y)`, `@sqrt`, `@exp`, `@ln`, `@log`, `@pi`
- Rounding: `@floor`, `@ceil`, `@rnd`, `@round(value, n)` — round to n decimals
- Trig: `@sin`, `@cos`, `@tan`, `@asin`, `@acos`, `@atan`, `@atan2`
- `@dtr`/`@rtd` — degrees↔radians
- `@hypot(a,b)` — sqrt(a²+b²)
- Comparisons: `@max(a,b,c,...)`, `@min(a,b,c,...)` (multi-arg)
- `@if(cond, true_val, false_val)` — numeric or string
- `@nval("B", 5)` — numeric value of cell B5 by name
- `@ston("123")` — string to number

**Practice:** Column A: product names. Column B: prices. Column C: quantities. Column D: `=B*C` (subtotal). Row below: `=@sum(D0:D9)`. Add `=@if(B>100,"Expensive","Cheap")` in column E.

---

### Section 11 — String Expressions & Functions
- String formulas entered with `\"` (backslash + double-quote)
- Concatenation with `#`: `\"A0 # " " # B0`
- `@substr(str, start, end)`, `@upper(str)`, `@lower(str)`, `@capital(str)`
- `@replace(str, old, new)`, `@slen(str)`, `@eqs(a, b)`
- `@sval("A", 5)` — string value from cell A5
- `@coltoa(n)` — column number to letter (4 → "D")
- `@fmt("format", number)` — printf-style number→string
- `@evaluate(str)` — evaluate string as formula
- `@sevaluate(str)` — evaluate string as string formula
- `@ext("command", value)` — call external program
- `@lua("script", 1)` — call Lua script

**Practice:** Column A: first names, Column B: last names. Column C: `\"A0 # " " # B0`. Try `@upper` on a cell. Use `@substr` to extract initials.

---

### Section 12 — Dates & Times
- Dates stored as seconds since epoch (Dec 31, 1969)
- Entry method 1: `\12/03/2020` then `C-d` (uses locale's D_FMT)
- Entry method 2: `\12/03/2020` then `:datefmt "%d/%m/%Y"`
- Entry method 3: `=@dts(2020, 3, 12)` — year, month, day → timestamp
- `@dts(y, m, d)` or `@dts(m, d, y)` (auto-detects which format)
- `@tts(h, m, s)` — time to seconds since midnight
- `@now` — current timestamp
- Extraction: `@year`, `@month`, `@day`, `@hour`, `@minute`, `@second`
- Display: `@date(timestamp)` or `@date(timestamp, "%d/%m/%Y")`
- Format: `:format "d%b %Y"` → "Mar 2020"
- strftime specifiers: `%d`, `%m`, `%Y`, `%H`, `%M`, `%S`, `%b`, `%B`, `%a`, `%A`, etc.
- `:datefmt "%d/%m/%Y"` — set default date display format

**Practice:** Enter 3 dates, convert with C-d, format as different styles. Create a cell with `=@now` and format it. Calculate age from a birthdate.

---

### Section 13 — Number Formatting
- `:format "format_string"` on selected cell/range
- Placeholders: `#` (optional digit), `0` (zero-padded digit)
- Decimal point: `.`
- Thousands separator: `,`
- Percentage: `%` (multiplies display by 100)
- Scientific: `E+`, `E-`, `e+`, `e-`
- Date: `d` first character → strftime format
- Examples:
  - `:format "###,###.00"` → "1,234.56"
  - `:format "0000.00"` → "0034.50"
  - `:format "##0.00%"` → "75.00%"
  - `:format "d%d/%m/%Y"` → "14/03/2023"
- `:formatcol width precision fixed-point` — per-column defaults
- `:formatrow height`
- `:unformat` — remove cell format
- **Note:** The cell's internal value is unchanged — only display changes

**Practice:** Format a column as currency ($#,##0.00 style), another as percentage, another as date. Use `:unformat` to revert.

---

### Section 14 — Sorting & Filtering
- **Sorting:**
  - `:sort range "sort_string"`
  - Each criterion: `±``#`/`$``column_letter`
  - `+` = ascending, `-` = descending
  - `#` = sort by numeric value, `$` = sort by string
  - Multiple criteria separated by `;`
  - Examples: `:sort A0:C100 "+#C"` (ascending by numeric column C), `:sort A0:C100 "+#C;-#B"` (by C asc, then B desc)
  - If range is selected, just `:sort "sort_string"`

- **Filtering:**
  - `:addfilter "#B<8000"`
  - `:addfilter "@eqs(@substr($B,1,3),'SEP')"`
  - `:addfilter "#B>3000;#B<5000"`
  - `:showfilters` — list filters with IDs
  - `:filteron range` or `:filteron` (uses selection)
  - `:filteroff` — remove filter
  - `:delfilter 0` — delete filter #0; `:delfilters` — delete all

**Practice:** Create customer data (name, amount, date). Sort by amount descending. Filter to show only amounts > 1000.

---

### Section 15 — Autofill, Formula Copy, Subtotal
- `:fill A0:A100 1 0.5` — fill 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5...
- `:fcopy` — copy formula down until empty cell to the left
- `:fcopy columns` — copy all formulas in first selected row down their columns
- `:fcopy rows` — copy all formulas in first selected column right along their rows
- `:fcopy cells` — copy first cell's formula to all selected cells
- `:fsum` — sum values above (vertical) or left (horizontal) until non-numeric
- `:subtotal group_col @operation value_col` — like GROUP BY + aggregate
  - Operations: `@sum`, `@prod`, `@avg`, `@count`, `@stddev`, `@max`, `@min`
  - Example: `:subtotal A @sum C` on A0:C9 → sums C grouped by A

**Practice:** Numbers 1-100 in column A. Use `:fill` for 1-100. Add formula `=A0*2` in B0, then `:fcopy` to fill column B. Try `:fsum` below a column.

---

### Section 16 — Lookups & Indexing
- `@lookup(search_range, expr)` — find value, return adjacent cell value
  - Range must be single row or single column
  - String: exact match; Numeric: last ≤ value (range must be sorted asc)
- `@hlookup(range_or_expr, expr_or_range, offset)` — horizontal lookup with offset
- `@vlookup(range_or_expr, expr_or_range, offset)` — vertical lookup with offset
- `@index(range, n)` — nth item (1-based) from single row/col range
- `@index(range, row, col)` — row, col into 2D range
- `@stindex` — like `@index` but returns string value
- Crosstab example with `@hlookup` + `@vlookup`

**Practice:** Create a price lookup table (item→price). Use @lookup to find a price. Create a grid and use @index to retrieve values.

---

### Section 17 — Multiple Sheets
- `:newsheet "Expenses"` — create new sheet
- `:renamesheet "Income"` — rename current
- `:delsheet "Expenses"` or `:delsheet` (current)
- `gt` / `gT` or `:nextsheet` / `:prevsheet`
- `:movetosheet "Income"` — switch to named sheet
- `:movesheet 2` — reorder (0-based)
- Cross-sheet references: `={"Sheet2"}!A0 + {"Sheet2"}!B0`
- Range across sheets: `@sum({"Sheet2"}!A0:{"Sheet2"}!A10)`
- `@stindex` example with cross-sheet lookups

**Practice:** Create Sheet1 ("Summary"), Sheet2 ("Jan"), Sheet3 ("Feb"). Reference Jan/Feb totals in Summary sheet.

---

### Section 18 — Configuration & Customization
- Config file: `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc` (loaded at startup)
- `:set` — view all options
- `:set option=value`; `:set option` (toggle on); `:set nooption` (toggle off)
- Key options:
  - `autocalc` — auto-recalculate formulas
  - `numeric` — digits start numeric entry (vs command multiplier)
  - `numeric_zero` / `numeric_decimal` — 0 or `.` start entry
  - `newline_action` — `j` (down), `l` (right), `0` (stay) after entry
  - `overlap` / `truncate` / `autowrap` — overflow behavior
  - `half_page_scroll` — C-f/C-b scroll half page
  - `ignorecase` — case-insensitive search
  - `xlsx_readformulas` — import formulas from XLSX
  - `ignore_hidden` — skip hidden rows on export
  - `show_cursor` — screen cursor follows cell
  - `command_timeout` / `mapping_timeout`
- Key mapping:
  - `:nmap "H" ":h<CR>"` — map H to :help in Normal
  - `:imap "kj" "<ESC>"` — kj = Escape in Insert
  - `:nnoremap` (non-recursive), `:nunmap`
  - `<CR>`, `<ESC>`, `<TAB>`, `<C-x>`, `<LEFT>`, etc. as special keys

**Practice:** Create `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc` with `set autowrap=1`, `set numeric=1`, `set newline_action=j`. Add a custom key mapping.

---

### Section 19 — Colors & Themes
- `:color "type=HEADINGS fg=WHITE bg=CYAN bold=1"`
- Affected types: HEADINGS, NUMB, STRG, EXPRESSION, CELL_ERROR, CELL_NEGATIVE, CELL_SELECTION, CELL_SELECTION_SC, GRID_EVEN/ODD, etc.
- Colors: WHITE, BLACK, RED, GREEN, YELLOW, BLUE, MAGENTA, CYAN, DEFAULT_COLOR, NONE_COLOR
- `:cellcolor "bg=CYAN fg=WHITE bold=1"` — color specific cell(s)
- `:cellcolor A2:A6 "bg=CYAN fg=WHITE"`
- `:define_color "skyblue" 75 50 200` — custom RGB color (needs 256-color terminal)
- `:redefine_color "RED" 250 0 0` — change ncurses color
- Built-in themes in `/themes`: dracula, old.sc, papercolor-dark, prince.persia
- Load theme: `:load path/to/theme/themename`
- Persist colors in `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc`

**Practice:** Apply colors to headings, define a custom color, try loading a theme file.

---

### Section 20 — Searching
- `/4` — find cell containing number 4
- `/"hello"` — find cell containing string "hello"
- `/"[_mente]"` — regex: any char followed by "mente"
- `/#"[0-9]+"` — regex on formatted numbers
- `/%"A0"` — regex on expressions (find cells referencing A0)
- `?` — search backwards
- `n` — next match; `N` — previous match
- `:int goto B2` — navigate to cell B2

**Practice:** Create data with various values. Search for a number, a string, then a regex pattern. Use `n`/`N` to cycle through matches.

---

### Section 21 — Plotting with gnuplot
- Requires gnuplot installed
- Select data range, then `:plot line` / `:plot scatter` / `:plot bar` / `:plot pie`
- Default terminal is "dumb" (ASCII art); "caca" recommended for color
- `:plotedit scatter` — edit the gnuplot script
- Plot templates in `$HOME/.config/sc-im/` or `/usr/local/share/sc-im/`
- Customize terminal, output to image, etc.

**Practice:** Create a simple dataset (months vs values), select it, plot as bar chart.

---

### Section 22 — Command-line / Non-interactive Mode
- `sc-im --nocurses` — no TUI, for scripting
- `--quit_afterload` — quit after loading, not interactive
- `--export_csv`, `--export_tab`, `--export_mkd`, `--export_txt`
- `--output=result.txt` — write output to file
- `--quiet` — suppress messages
- `--version` — show version and features
- Pipeline: `cat script | sc-im --nocurses`
- Script examples:
  ```
  let A0=10
  let A1=20
  let A2=A0+A1
  recalc
  getnum A2
  ```
- Script commands: `LET`, `LABEL`, `EXECUTE`, `EXPORT`, `RECALC`, `GETNUM`, `GETSTRING`, `QUIT`

**Practice:** Write a small script, pipe it to sc-im, capture the output. Export a file to CSV non-interactively.

---

### Section 23 — Complete Project: Personal Finance Tracker
Step-by-step build of a budget spreadsheet:
1. Create sheets: "Summary", "Jan", "Feb", "Mar"
2. Set up columns: Date, Description, Category, Amount, Type (Income/Expense)
3. Enter sample data for 3 months
4. Add SUMIF-style totals with conditional @sum
5. Summary sheet pulls from all month sheets
6. Format as currency and dates
7. Freeze header rows
8. Add cell colors for negative values
9. Sort and filter
10. Optionally plot spending by category

## Module 0: What is sc-im?

**sc-im** (Spreadsheet Calculator Improved) is a terminal-based spreadsheet program forked from the classic `sc` originally written by James Gosling.

### Key Characteristics

| Property     | Value                             |
| ------------ | --------------------------------- |
| **Interface**    | ncurses terminal (no GUI)         |
| **Keybindings**  | Vim-style (`hjkl`, modes, etc.)     |
| **Grid size**    | 702 columns × 999 rows default    |
| **File formats** | `.sc` (native), CSV, TSV, XLSX, ODS |

### Why sc-im for quick calculations?

- **Speed**: No GUI overhead - opens instantly
- **Keyboard-first**: Never touch your mouse
- **Remote-friendly**: Works over SSH, in tmux, on servers
- **Vim integration**: If you know vim, sc-im feels natural

### How it differs from Excel/LibreOffice Calc

| Excel/Calc              | sc-im                                     |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Point-and-click         | Keyboard-driven                           |
| GUI with toolbars       | Terminal interface                        |
| `=SUM(A1:A10)`            | `=@sum(A1:A10)` (functions prefixed with `@`) |
| Visual formatting first | Data entry first                          |

### The Vim Connection

sc-im borrowed vim's **modal editing** philosophy:

- **Normal mode**: Navigate and execute commands (default)
- **Insert mode**: Put values into cells
- **Edit mode**: Modify existing cell contents  
- **Visual mode**: Select ranges
- **Command mode**: File operations (`:w`, `:q`)

---

### Before Module 1

**Reflect:** Think about a calculation task you do often. It could be:
- Budget tracking
- Converting units
- averaging numbers
- Simple inventory counts

We'll build a practical example using this as we learn.

## Module 1: Installation & First Launch

### Step 1 — Install sc-im

**On Ubuntu/Debian:**
```bash
sudo apt update && sudo apt install sc-im
```

**On macOS (Homebrew):**
```bash
brew install sc-im
```

**From source (if needed):**
```bash
git clone https://github.com/andmarti1424/sc-im.git
cd sc-im/src && make && sudo make install
```

### Step 2 — Launch

Open a terminal and type:
```bash
sc-im
```
This opens a **blank spreadsheet**.

To open an existing file:
```bash
sc-im mybudget.sc
```

### Step 3 — Understand the Interface

When you launch, you'll see:

```
   |  A  |  B  |  C  |  D  |
<!-- table not formatted: invalid structure -->
-- +-----+-----+-----+-----+
 1 |
 2 |
 3 |
```

- **Top row**: Column labels (`A`, `B`, `C`...)
- **Left column**: Row numbers (`1`, `2`, `3`...)
- **Highlighted cell**: Your cursor position (shown with cyan/red highlight)
- **Bottom status bar**: Shows current cell address, content, and mode

### Step 4 — The Status Bar

The bottom line shows:
```
A1  "  [NORMAL]  
```
- **`A1`** = Current cell address
- **`"`** = Cell is empty (would show `'` for numeric)
- **`[NORMAL]`** = Current mode

---

### Quick Launch Commands

| Command               | Effect                 |
| --------------------- | ---------------------- |
| `sc-im`                 | Open blank spreadsheet |
| `sc-im file.sc`         | Open existing `.sc` file |
| `sc-im -c csv file.csv` | Import CSV             |

---

**Practice tasks:**
1. Install sc-im (use your package manager or source)
2. Launch it with `sc-im`
3. Notice the layout — find column headers, row numbers, status bar
4. Quit by typing `:q` and pressing Enter

**After quitting, notice:** The screen clears and you return to bash — sc-im is lightweight like that.

## Module 2: Navigation Fundamentals

### The Core Keys: `hjkl`

Like vim, sc-im uses `hjkl` for directional movement:

| Key | Direction |
| --- | --------- |
| `h`   | Left      |
| `j`   | Down      |
| `k`   | Up        |
| `l`   | Right     |

**Mnemonic:** `h` is leftmost, `l` is rightmost, `j` points down.

### Arrow Keys

Arrow keys also work: `←↓↑→`

If you're vim-trained, stick with `hjkl` — it's faster.

### Page Navigation

| Key       | Action              |
| --------- | ------------------- |
| `Ctrl+f`    | Page down           |
| `Ctrl+b`    | Page up             |
| `Space`     | Move right (like `l`) |
| `Backspace` | Move left (like `h`)  |

### Jump to Specific Cells

Type the cell address directly:
```
goA12<Enter>
```
This jumps directly to cell **A12**.

You can also prefix with a number for quick row/column access:
- `g0<Enter>` — jump to column A of current row
- `g$<Enter>` — jump to last column

### Row/Column Boundaries

| Key | Action                |
| --- | --------------------- |
| `gg`  | Go to first cell (A1) |
| `G`   | Go to last row        |
| `0`   | Jump to column A      |
| `$`   | Jump to last column   |

### Tab Navigation

| Key       | Action              |
| --------- | ------------------- |
| `Tab`       | Move right (like `l`) |
| `Shift+Tab` | Move left (like `h`)  |

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Open sc-im with a blank spreadsheet
2. Using only `hjkl`, navigate from A1 to Z26 (diagonal)
3. Use `go` command to jump directly to cell Z100, then back to A1
4. Press `Ctrl+f` several times to page down, then `Ctrl+b` to page up
5. Navigate using vim keys with your eyes closed (muscle memory)

---

## Module 3: Entering Data

### Three Types of Cell Content

| Type     | Prefix Key | Example       |
| -------- | ---------- | ------------- |
| **Numbers**  | `=`          | `=42`           |
| **Text**     | `\`          | `\Hello`        |
| **Formulas** | `=` + `@`      | `=@sum(A1:A10)` |

### Entering Numbers

1. Press `=` to enter numeric mode
2. Type your number (e.g., `42`)
3. Press `Enter`

```
=42<Enter>
```

The status bar shows `'` to indicate a numeric value.

### Entering Text

1. Press `\` to enter text mode  
2. Type your text (e.g., `Hello World`)
3. Press `Enter`

```
\Hello World<Enter>
```

The status bar shows `"` to indicate a string/text value.

### Entering Formulas

Formulas always start with `=`:

```sc-im
=100+50        Basic arithmetic
=@sum(A1:A10)  Sum function
=A1+B1         Cell references in formulas
```

Press `=` then type your formula, then `Enter`.

### Canceling Entry

If you make a mistake while typing:
- Press `Escape` to cancel and keep the old value
- Or press `Ctrl+c`

This is **vim-style**: Escape cancels insert/edit operations.

### Quick Numeric Entry Trick

When entering many numbers in a column, after pressing `=` and Enter on a number, the cursor automatically moves down — ready for the next entry. This speeds up data entry significantly:

```
=100<Enter>   # Cursor moves to cell below
150<Enter>    # Just type the number (no = needed)
200<Enter>    # Same - still in numeric mode after first =
250<Enter>    # Continue...
```

The `=` is only needed for the **first** cell if you're entering consecutive numbers downward.

### Text Alignment Prefixes

When entering text, you can prefix with a justification character:

| Prefix  | Effect             |
| ------- | ------------------ |
| `<`       | Left-justified     |
| `\` `>`     | Right-justified    |
| `\` alone | Centered (default) |

Example: `\>Hello` enters "Hello" right-aligned.

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Create this table:

```
   A       B      C
1 Name     Age    City
2 Alice    30     NYC
3 Bob      25     LA
4 Carol    35     Chicago
```

2. Navigate to each cell and enter the values using `\` for text, `=` for numbers

3. Notice how status bar changes between numeric (`'`) and string (`"`) indicators

---

## Module 4: Editing & Deleting Cells

### Viewing Cell Content

When cursor is on a cell:
- **Status bar** shows first few characters of the content
- Press `,` (comma) to toggle between showing values vs formulas in the grid

### Edit Mode: `e` and `E`

If a cell already has content and you want to change it:

| Key | Purpose                           |
| --- | --------------------------------- |
| `e`   | Edit numeric value (vi-style)     |
| `E`   | Edit string/text value (vi-style) |

These work like vim's `c` (change) command — they delete the current content and put you in insert mode.

**Example:** Cell A1 contains `100`. You want to change it to `200`:
```
e        # Delete 100, enter edit mode
200<Enter>
```

### Deleting Cell Content: `x`

Press `x` to delete the **entire contents** of the current cell:

```
A1 has value 100
x     # Cell is now empty
```

This works on both numeric and string cells.

### Undo and Redo

| Key    | Action |
| ------ | ------ |
| `u`      | Undo   |
| `Ctrl+r` | Redo   |

Undo is your safety net — use it liberally.

**Example:**
```
=x<Enter>        # Enter 100
u               # Undo - cell is empty again
Ctrl+r          # Redo - 100 is back
```

### Row and Column Deletion

| Key | Action                |
| --- | --------------------- |
| `dr`  | Delete current row    |
| `dc`  | Delete current column |

**Example:** You're on row 5. Press `dr`. Row 5 disappears, all rows below shift up.

### Clearing a Cell vs Deleting

| Key | Effect                                           |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| `x`   | Clear cell content                               |
| `dd`  | Delete entire row                                |
| `D`   | Delete cell content and formatting (normal mode) |

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Enter a number in A1, then use `e` to change it to something else
2. Enter text in B1, then use `E` to edit it
3. Use `x` to delete cells and `u` to undo the deletion
4. Create a row of 5 numbers, then delete the middle one with `dr`
5. Try `Ctrl+r` (redo) after undoing several operations

---

## Module 5: Formulas Deep Dive

### Formula Syntax Rules

1. All formulas start with `=`
2. Functions are prefixed with `@` (e.g., `@sum`, not `SUM`)
3. Cell references use the format `A1`, `B2`, etc.
4. Ranges use colon syntax: `A1:A10`

**Excel comparison:**
| Excel    | sc-im     |
| -------- | --------- |
| `=SUM(A1)` | `=@sum(A1)` |
| `=A1+B1`   | `=A1+B1`    |
| `=A1*2`    | `=A1*2`     |

### Arithmetic Operators

sc-im supports standard arithmetic:

| Operator | Meaning        | Example     |
| -------- | -------------- | ----------- |
| `+`        | Addition       | `=10+5` → 15  |
| `-`        | Subtraction    | `=10-3` → 7   |
| `*`        | Multiplication | `=6*7` → 42   |
| `/`        | Division       | `=100/4` → 25 |

### Cell References in Formulas

**Single cell reference:**
```
=A1        # Value of A1
```

**Arithmetic with cells:**
```
=A1+B1     # Add A1 and B1
=A1*2      # Double A1
=(A1+B1)/2 # Average of A1 and B1
```

### Range Syntax

Ranges define a rectangular block:

| Syntax | Meaning                                        |
| ------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| `A1:A10` | All cells from A1 to A10 (column A, rows 1-10) |
| `A1:D4`  | Rectangle: columns A-D, rows 1-4               |
| `B2:B20` | Column B, rows 2-20                            |

### Using Ranges in Formulas

**Sum a column:**
```
=@sum(A1:A100)
```

**Average a range:**
```
=@avg(B1:B10)
```

**Multiply each cell in range by 2 (element-wise):**
```
=A1:A10*2
```

### Auto-calculation

By default, formulas recalculate when any referenced cell changes. You can also trigger manual recalc:

| Key | Action          |
| --- | --------------- |
| `=`   | Recalculate all |

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Create a simple budget table:
```
   A          B
1  Income    5000
2  Rent      1200
3  Food      400
4  Transport 200
5  Savings   ?
```

2. Put formulas in B5 to calculate: `=B1-B2-B3-B4`
3. Change B1 (Income) and watch B5 update automatically
4. Create a multiplication table using cell references like `=A1*B$1`

---

## Module 6: Essential Functions

### Function Syntax Reminder

All functions in sc-im start with `@`:

```
=@function_name(arguments)
```

### The Big Four Statistical Functions

**`@sum(range)` — Sum**
```
=@sum(A1:A10)     # Adds all values in A1 through A10
```

**`@avg(range)` — Average**
```
=@avg(B1:B5)      # (B1+B2+B3+B4+B5)/5
```

**`@min(range)` — Minimum**
```
=@min(C1:C100)    # Smallest value in range
```

**`@max(range)` — Maximum**
```
=@max(C1:C100)    # Largest value in range
```

### Counting Functions

**`@count(range)` — Count numeric cells**
```
=@count(A1:A50)   # How many cells contain numbers (not empty or text)
```

**`@rows(range)` / `@cols(range)`**
```
=@rows(A1:D10)    # Returns 10
=@cols(A1:D10)    # Returns 4
```

### The `@if()` Conditional Function

Format: `=@if(condition, true_value, false_value)`

**Example:**
```
=@if(A1>100, "High", "Low")   # If A1 > 100, show "High", else "Low"
```

Comparison operators:
| Operator | Meaning          |
| -------- | ---------------- |
| `<`        | Less than        |
| `>`        | Greater than     |
| `<=`       | Less or equal    |
| `>=`       | Greater or equal |
| `=`        | Equal            |

### Nested Functions

Functions can be nested:
```
=@sum(A1:@max(B1:B10))    # Sum from A1 to the cell address returned by @max
```

More practically:
```
=@avg(A1:A10)             # Average of 10 numbers
=@min(A1:@avg(A1:A10)*0.5) # Find min in a calculated range
```

### Numeric Formatting

| Key  | Action                  |
| ---- | ----------------------- |
| `:%`   | Format as percentage    |
| `:e`   | Scientific notation     |
| `:.2f` | 2 decimal places (`:.nf`) |

Example: In edit mode, prefix number with format:
```
:.2f3.14159<Enter>    # Displays as 3.14
```

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Enter 10 random numbers in column A (A1:A10)
2. Use `@sum(A1:A10)` to get the total
3. Use `@avg(A1:A10)` for the average
4. Use `@min` and `@max` to find the range
5. Create a grade sheet where:
   - Column A has student names
   - Column B has scores (0-100)
   - Column C shows `=@if(B1>=60,"Pass","Fail")`

---

## Module 7: File Operations

### Saving Your Work

| Command        | Action                    |
| -------------- | ------------------------- |
| `:w`             | Save current file         |
| `:w filename.sc` | Save as specific filename |

**Example:**
```
:w mybudget.sc<Enter>
```

The `.sc` extension is sc-im's native format.

### Opening Files

From the **command line** (before launching):
```bash
sc-im mybudget.sc
```

From **inside sc-im**:
```
:e mybudget.sc<Enter>
```

Note: `:e` in sc-im means "edit" or open, not "exit" (vim convention).

### Exporting to Other Formats

| Command      | Action          |
| ------------ | --------------- |
| `:w file.csv`  | Export as CSV   |
| `:w file.tsv`  | Export as TSV   |
| `:w file.xls`  | Export as XLS   |
| `:e file.xlsx` | Open Excel file |

**To export your current spreadsheet to CSV:**
```
:w backup.csv<Enter>
```

### Importing Files

Open a CSV directly:
```bash
sc-im -c csv data.csv
```

The `-c csv` flag tells sc-im the file format.

### Quitting

| Command | Action                      |
| ------- | --------------------------- |
| `:q`      | Quit (prompts if unsaved)   |
| `:q!`     | Force quit (ignore unsaved) |
| `:wq`     | Save and quit               |

---

### Auto-save Warning

sc-im **does not auto-save**. Get into the habit of:
- Saving frequently with `:w`
- Using `:wq` when done to save and exit in one step

### The `.sc` File Format

Native `.sc` files store:
- Cell values and formulas
- Formatting (width, justification)
- Configuration per sheet

CSV/XLSX exports may lose formatting — use native format for saving projects.

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Create a small spreadsheet with data
2. Save it as `test.sc` using `:w test.sc`
3. Quit with `:q`
4. Reopen the file: `sc-im test.sc`
5. Make a change, save again with `:w`
6. Export to CSV: `:w test.csv`, then quit and check the CSV

---

## Module 8: Copy, Paste & Cell Operations

### Yank (Copy) and Paste

| Key | Action                   |
| --- | ------------------------ |
| `yy`  | Yank (copy) current cell |
| `p`   | Paste after cursor       |
| `P`   | Paste before cursor      |

**Example:**
```
yy       # Copy A1
j        # Move to A2
p        # Paste into A2 (now A2 = A1)
```

### Yank and Paste with Adjustment

When you paste, cell references in formulas can auto-adjust:

| Key | Action                           |
| --- | -------------------------------- |
| `Pc`  | Paste and adjust cell references |

**Example:**
```
A1 formula: =B1      # References B1
yy                 # Copy A1
j                  # Move to A2
Pc                 # Paste into A2, adjusts to =B2 (references shifted down)
```

### Inserting Rows and Columns

| Key | Action                        |
| --- | ----------------------------- |
| `ir`  | Insert row above current      |
| `5ir` | Insert 5 rows above current   |
| `ic`  | Insert column left of current |

**Example:** You're on A5:
```
ir       # New blank row inserted at row 5, old row 5 becomes row 6
```

### Deleting Rows and Columns

| Key | Action                |
| --- | --------------------- |
| `dr`  | Delete current row    |
| `dc`  | Delete current column |

**Example:**
```
dr       # Delete entire current row, rows below shift up
```

### Visual Selection Mode

Press `v` to enter visual mode, then use movement keys to select a range:

1. Move cursor to start cell (e.g., A1)
2. Press `v`
3. Use `hjkl` or arrows to extend selection
4. Perform operation on selected range

**Example — Copy a block:**
```
v        # Enter visual mode
jjll     # Select 2 rows x 3 columns
yy       # Yank the entire selection
j        # Move cursor
p        # Paste block
```

### Range Operations in Visual Mode

After selecting with `v`, you can:
| Key | Action                |
| --- | --------------------- |
| `x`   | Delete selection      |
| `y`   | Yank (copy) selection |

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Create a column of 5 numbers
2. Copy the column to a second column using `yy` and `p`
3. Insert two new rows at row 3
4. Delete one of those rows with `dr`
5. Use visual mode (`v`) to select a 3x3 block and yank it
6. Paste the block elsewhere

---

## Module 9: Modes Mastery

### The Five Modes

sc-im has five distinct modes, each with different key behaviors:

| Mode    | Purpose                            |
| ------- | ---------------------------------- |
| **NORMAL**  | Default — navigation and commands  |
| **INSERT**  | Enter values via `=` or `\           |
| **EDIT**    | Modify existing cell content (`e`,`E`) |
| **VISUAL**  | Range selection with movement      |
| **COMMAND** | File ops, help (`:w`, `:q`, `:h`)        |

### Mode Indicators

The status bar at the bottom shows current mode:
```
A1  "  [NORMAL]
```

When in INSERT or EDIT mode, you may also see an indicator like `-- INSERT --` or `-- EDIT --`.

### Normal Mode

This is your **default resting state**. Most of your time is here.

Keys do commands (not insert characters):
- `h j k l` — movement
- `x` — delete cell
- `u` — undo
- `yy` — yank

### Insert Mode

Entered by pressing `=` or `\`:

| Key | Effect                       |
| --- | ---------------------------- |
| `=`   | Begin entering numeric value |
| `\`   | Begin entering text value    |

In this mode, everything you type goes into the cell. Press Enter to confirm.

### Edit Mode

Entered by pressing `e` or `E`:

| Key | Effect                               |
| --- | ------------------------------------ |
| `e`   | Edit current numeric cell (vi-style) |
| `E`   | Edit current string cell             |

Works like vim's `c` command — deletes existing content and enters insert mode.

### Visual Mode

Entered by pressing `v`:

- Movement keys extend the selection
- Press `y` to yank, `x` to delete
- Press Escape or `v` again to exit

### Command Mode

Entered by pressing `:`:

| Command     | Action            |
| ----------- | ----------------- |
| `:w`          | Save              |
| `:q`          | Quit              |
| `:h`          | Open help         |
| `:set option` | Set configuration |

Press Enter to execute, Escape to cancel.

### Mode Transitions

```
NORMAL  --(= or \)--> INSERT
NORMAL  --(e or E)--> EDIT  
NORMAL  --(v)------> VISUAL
NORMAL  --(:)------> COMMAND

INSERT  --(Enter)---> NORMAL (after value entered)
EDIT    --(Enter)---> NORMAL (after editing confirmed)
VISUAL  --(v or Esc)-> NORMAL
COMMAND --(Enter)---> NORMAL (command executed)
```

### The Escape Key in All Modes

**Escape always returns to Normal mode:**
- In INSERT: cancels current input, keeps old value
- In EDIT: cancels edit, keeps old value  
- In VISUAL: exits visual selection
- In COMMAND: cancels command entry

**This is identical to vim behavior.**

---

### Practice Exercises

1. Open sc-im and observe the status bar — you're in NORMAL mode
2. Press `=` and notice `[INSERT]` appears — type a number, Enter to confirm (back to NORMAL)
3. Press `\`, notice [INSERT] again — enter text, Escape to cancel (cell unchanged)
4. Enter a value, press `e` or `E` to edit it in EDIT mode
5. Use `:h` to open help, browse briefly, then `:q` to close help
6. Practice rapid switching between modes until muscle memory develops

---

## Module 10: Next Steps & Resources

### Configuration File

Create `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc` for persistent settings:

```sc-im
set autocalc           # Auto-recalculate formulas (default on)
set numeric            # Default to numeric mode for = key
set numeric_decimal=2  # Show 2 decimal places
```

**Useful set options:**

| Option                | Effect                        |
| --------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| `set autocalc`          | Recalc after every change     |
| `set autoindent`        | Indent in edit mode           |
| `set numeric_decimal=N` | Default N decimal places      |
| `set xlsx_readformulas` | Read formulas from XLSX files |

### Column Width Adjustment

In command mode:
```
:col A 15<Enter>    # Set column A width to 15
```

Or use the mouse (if supported) — click and drag column borders.

### Additional Resources

**Official:**
- GitHub: https://github.com/andmarti1424/sc-im
- Built-in help: `:h` inside sc-im
- Man page: `man sc-im`


### Advanced Topics to Explore

When comfortable with basics:

| **Multiple sheets**    | Workbook with multiple tabs                 |
| **Custom keybindings** | Remap keys in `.scimrc`                       |

Here's a comprehensive beginner's guide to **sc-im**:

# SC-IM: Spreadsheet Calculator Improvised - Complete Beginner Guide

## 1. What is sc-im?

**sc-im** (Spreadsheet Calculator Improved) is a terminal-based spreadsheet program with vim-like keybindings. It's an enhanced fork of the classic `sc` (Spreadsheet Calculator) originally written by James Gosling.

| Property       | Description                                |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| **Type**           | Terminal/ncurses spreadsheet application   |
| **Key Style**      | Vim-like (hjkl navigation, modes)          |
| **Base**           | Forked from `sc`                             |
| **Platform**       | Linux, macOS, BSD (Windows via WSL)        |
| **Format Support** | CSV, TSV, XLSX, ODS, and native `.sc` format |

**Use cases**: Ideal for users who live in the terminal, prefer keyboard-driven workflows, or need spreadsheet functionality on remote servers.

---

## 2. Basic Concepts

### Cells
- The fundamental unit - each cell holds a value (number, text, or formula)
- Referenced by column letter + row number (e.g., `A1`, `BC42`)

### Columns & Rows
- **Columns**: Letter-labeled (A, B, C... Z, AA, AB...)
- **Rows**: Number-labeled (1, 2, 3...)
- Default grid: 702 columns × 999 rows

### Navigation
- The cursor highlights the current cell
- You can move by cell, by page, or jump directly to any cell address

---

## 3. Installation

### Ubuntu/Debian
```bash
sudo apt install sc-im
```

### macOS (Homebrew)
```bash
brew install sc-im
```

### From Source (Linux)
```bash
# Install dependencies
sudo apt install ncurses-dev bison flex gcc make pkg-config

# Clone and build
git clone https://github.com/andmarti1424/sc-im.git
cd sc-im/src
make
sudo make install
```

### Build with Lua scripting support (macOS)
```bash
brew install lua@5.1
# Then follow source build instructions
```

---

## 4. Core Keybindings

### Quick Reference

| Key        | Action                               |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------ |
| `hjkl`       | Move left/down/up/right (vim-style)  |
| Arrow keys | Move cursor                          |
| `=`          | Enter numeric value or formula       |
| `\`          | Enter text value                     |
| `e`          | Edit current cell (numeric mode)     |
| `E`          | Edit current cell (string/text mode) |
| `x`          | Delete cell content                  |
| `u`          | Undo                                 |
| `Ctrl+r`     | Redo                                 |
| `yy`         | Copy cell                            |
| `p`          | Paste                                |
| `v` + hjkl   | Select range (visual selection)      |
| `:q`         | Quit                                 |
| `:h`         | Help                                 |
| `:w file.sc` | Save                                 |

### Row/Column Operations

| Key | Action                        |
| --- | ----------------------------- |
| `ir`  | Insert row above current      |
| `ic`  | Insert column left of current |
| `dr`  | Delete current row            |
| `dc`  | Delete current column         |
| `5ir` | Insert 5 rows                 |

### Navigation Shortcuts

| Key    | Action                        |
| ------ | ----------------------------- |
| `goA12`  | Jump directly to cell A12     |
| `Ctrl+f` | Page down                     |
| `Ctrl+b` | Page up                       |
| `0`      | Go to column A of current row |
| `$`      | Go to last column             |

---

## 5. Basic Operations

### Entering Data

**Numbers**: Press `=` then type the number, then Enter
```
=42<Enter>
```

**Text**: Press `\` then type text, then Enter
```
\Hello World<Enter>
```

**Formulas**: Press `=` then type formula (prefixed with @)
```
=@sum(A1:A10)<Enter>
```

### Editing Cells

| Key | Purpose                                       |
| --- | --------------------------------------------- |
| `e`   | Edit numeric value in current cell (vi-style) |
| `E`   | Edit string/text value in current cell        |

### Text Justification
- `<` prefix: Left-justified text
- `>` prefix: Right-justified text  
- `\` alone: Centered text

### Copy/Paste
```bash
yy      # Yank (copy) current cell
p       # Paste after cursor
Pc      # Paste and adjust cell references
```

### Visual Selection & Range Operations
1. Press `v` to enter visual mode
2. Use `hjkl` or arrow keys to extend selection
3. Perform operation (`yy`, `x`, etc.)

---

## 6. Formulas Basics

### Formula Syntax
Formulas start with `=` and use `@function()` syntax:

```sc-im
=@sum(A1:A10)     Sum of cells A1 through A10
=@avg(B1:B4)      Average of B1 through B4
=@min(C1:C20)     Minimum value in range
=@max(D1:D100)    Maximum value in range
=@count(E1:E50)   Count of numeric cells
```

### Cell References in Formulas
- `A1` - Single cell
- `A1:B10` - Range (A1 to B10)
- Can use arithmetic: `@sum(A1:A10)*2`
- Can reference other formulas: `@avg(A1:A5)+@min(B1:B5)`

### Mathematical Operators
```
+  Addition       =25+25
-  Subtraction    =50-10  
*  Multiplication =6*7
/  Division        =100/4
```

---

## 7. Modes Overview

sc-im has several modes like vim:

| Mode    | Purpose                          |
| ------- | -------------------------------- |
| **Normal**  | Default; navigation and commands |
| **Insert**  | Insert values (via `=`, `\`)         |
| **Edit**    | Edit cell contents (`e`, `E`)        |
| **Visual**  | Range selection (`v`)              |
| **Command** | Execute commands (`:w`, `:q`, etc.)  |

---

## 8. Configuration

Create `~/.config/sc-im/scimrc` to customize:

```sc-im
set autocalc          # Auto-recalculate formulas
set numeric           # Numeric mode for faster input
set numeric_decimal=2 # 2 decimal places
set overlap           # Allow overlapping cells
set xlsx_readformulas # Read formulas from XLSX
```

---

## 9. File Operations

| Command      | Action             |
| ------------ | ------------------ |
| `:w file.sc`   | Save as `.sc` format |
| `:w file.csv`  | Export as CSV      |
| `:e file.xlsx` | Export as Excel    |
| `:q`           | Quit               |

**Open a file**: `sc-im spreadsheet.sc`

---

- **Built-in Help**: Press `:h` inside sc-im
- **Wiki**: https://github.com/andmarti1424/sc-im/wiki

```
# Exit
```
